--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 sec: add GSSIAM nodemap integration and export setup

This patch introduces the target-side GSSIAM security context setup,
nodemap integration, and export binding:

- GSSIAM Nodemap Integration:
  GSSIAM uses a Hybrid RPC Model where Data RPCs use Null framing for
  performance. To enforce fine-grained access control (UID/GID
  squashing, project IDs, and fileset/subdirectory boundaries) on
  these Null-framed RPCs, a dynamic child nodemap is created for each
  unique GSSIAM identity (inheriting from a parent "gssiam" nodemap)
  and pinned to the export. The dynamic nodemap is automatically
  cleaned up when the export is closed or expires.

- GSSIAM Nodemap Naming & Hashing:
  Introduces gssiam_nodemap_name() and lustre_gssiam_hash_key() to
  generate a unique 64-bit FNV-1a hash for each identity based on its
  token, token length, subdirectory, project ID, and auth
  permissions, preventing boundary collisions between different
  identities.

- Upcall Cache Deferred Free Rework (Load-Bearing):
  Introduces gss_cleanup_wq workqueue and switches
  rsc_upcall_cache_ops to .free_delay (rsc_entry_free_delay() +
  rsc_entry_free_work()) to move __rsc_free() out from under
  write_lock(&cache->uc_lock) into workqueue (process) context for all
  GSS flavors. This is a load-bearing prerequisite for GSSIAM: it
  allows gssiam_delete_sec_context() to call nodemap_putref(), which
  may invoke nodemap_destroy() and acquire active_config_lock (a
  mutex) without violating atomic context.

- Target & Security Infrastructure:
  - Add sptlrpc_target_export_sec_install() to install the security
    context into the export during connection establishment.
  - Update sptlrpc_wireflavor2policy() to demand-load ptlrpc_gss for
    SPTLRPC_POLICY_GSSIAM as well as SPTLRPC_POLICY_GSS.
  - Export lustre_free_reply_state() for use by GSSIAM.
  - Add lustre_hash_fnv_1a_64_continue() incremental hashing helper.
  - In sptlrpc_process_config(), clean up the "gssiam" nodemap when no
    GSSIAM rules remain, outside of sptlrpc_conf_lock to prevent
    mutex deadlock.

Patch Set 42:

(11 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The main one is that gssiam_install_ctx() is wired into sptlrpc_target_export_sec_install() without the shared-static-context guard that every other gssiam sops callback has, so a Null-framed CONNECT carrying the gssiam flavor reaches it with req->rq_svc_ctx == &gssiam_svc_ctx.

The patch adds dynamic nodemap creation, refresh and teardown but Test-Parameters only asks for the existing sanity-sec list; should it also add a sanity-sec subtest that mounts with the gssiam flavor and checks the child nodemap is created with the expected projid/fileset and is removed again when the export goes away?

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-15 16:31:50.000000000) ---
Patch Set 64:

(8 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. Worth flagging up front that none of the new nodemap code added here can execute yet: gssiam_verify_target() unconditionally returns -EOPNOTSUPP, so gssiam_handle_init() now bails out before it ever reaches gssiam_get_nodemap(), and a GSSIAM handshake that completed on the parent commit now fails (with a CERROR per attempt) until the follow-on patch lands. The stub itself is described in the message, but the side effect is that gssiam_get_nodemap(), gssiam_install_ctx() and nodemap_export_refresh() land untested and unbisectable. It would help to say so explicitly, or to order the series so the verification lands first. Related: there is no test in this change - is a sanity-sec case that creates a GSSIAM identity, checks the dynamic nodemap appears, and checks it disappears again on disconnect planned for a later patch?

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-21 20:15:53.000000000) ---
Patch Set 70:

(5 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments.

The whole-patch worry is reachability: gssiam_verify_target() is a stub that always fails, and gssiam_handle_init() now treats that as fatal, so at this commit no GSSIAM SEC_CTX_INIT can succeed and none of the nodemap creation/installation code added here can ever run. That also means none of it is exercisable by a test, which is presumably why the patch adds none. Would it be cleaner to land the stub together with the implementation, or to keep gssiam_handle_init() working until the real verification exists?

The nodemap-lifecycle behavior this adds is user-visible and has no test coverage: `lctl nodemap_del` now refuses a GSSIAM-managed nodemap with -EPERM, and the per-identity dynamic nodemaps are created and torn down implicitly by connect/disconnect.
